v_{1}(X):=K_{1}v(X)-\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(X,X_{0})

satisfies all the assumption of the maximum principle (Lemma
14.33
) with
E=\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
and
F=B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/2)\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
. Indeed, since
v
is non-negative everywhere, it forces
\Tr v_{1}=\Tr v\geq 0
. We deduce that
v_{1}\geq 0
on
E
, i.e.

\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(X,X_{0})\leq K_{1}v(X)\qquad\text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4).

The claim (
15.51
) is now an immediate consequence of Lemma
15.28
.

We turn to the proof of (
15.52
). We first check that

(15.53)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\frac{13}{8}B\setminus\frac{11}{8}B.

Let
K
as in Lemma
15.14
. We want to establish (
15.53
) in the two sets:

(15.54)

\Omega_{1}:=\Omega\cap\{X\in B(x_{0},\frac{13}{8}r)\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{11}{8}r),\,\delta(X)<\frac{1}{8K}r\}

and

(15.55)

\Omega_{2}:=\{X\in B(x_{0},\frac{13}{8}r)\setminus B(x_{0},\frac{11}{8}r),\,\delta(X)\geq\frac{1}{8K}r\}.

The proof of (
15.53
) on
\Omega_{2}
is easy, it is only a consequence of the Harnack inequality (Lemma
11.35
) and the existence of Harnack chains.

Then, we prove (
15.53
) for

X\in\Omega_{1}
. Let thus
X\in\Omega_{1}
be given.
Take
x\in\Gamma
such that
|X-x|=\delta(X)
; in particular, particular,

|X-x|\leq\frac{r}{8}
, and hence

x\in\frac{7}{4}B
. Now let
X_{1}
be
a Corkscrew point for

(x,\frac{r}{4K})
.
Since
u
is a non-negative solution of
Lu=0
in
B(x,\frac{r}{4})\cap\Omega
satisfying
\Tr u=0
on
B(x,\frac{r}{4})\cap\Gamma
, Lemma
15.14
gives that
u(Y)\leq Cu(X_{1})
for

Y\in B(x,\frac{r}{4K})
and thus

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{1})
.
By the existence of Harnack chains (Proposition
2.18
) and the Harnack inequality (Lemma
11.35
),
u(X_{1})\leq Cu(X_{0})
. Hence
u(X)\leq u(X_{1})
, which completes
the proof of (
15.53
) on
\Omega_{1}

The end of the proof is as in
in
[
DFM2
, Lemma 11.117]
, but let us recall it. We proved (
15.53
) and now we want to get (
15.52
).
Recall from Lemma
15.1
that

\omega^{X}(\frac{7}{4}B\cap\Gamma)\geq C^{-1}
for

X\in\frac{13}{8}B\cap\Omega
.
Hence, by (
15.53
),

(15.56)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\frac{7}{4}B\cap\Gamma)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\left[\frac{13}{8}B\setminus\frac{11}{8}B\right]\cap\Omega.

Let
h\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B(x_{0},\frac{15}{8}r))
be such that
0\leq h\leq 1
and
h\equiv 1
on
B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r)
. Then let
u_{h}\in W
be the solution of
Lu_{h}=0
with the Dirichlet condition
\Tr u_{h}=\Tr h
.
By the positivity of the harmonic measure,

(15.57)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})u_{h}(X)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\left[\frac{13}{8}B\setminus\frac{11}{8}B\right]\cap\Omega.

The maximum principle given by Lemma
14.33
- where we take
E=\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\overline{\frac{11}{8}B}
and
F=\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\frac{13}{8}B
- yields

(15.58)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})u_{h}(X)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus\frac{13}{8}B

and hence

(15.59)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\omega^{X}(\Gamma_{2})\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus\frac{13}{8}B,

where we use again the positivity of the harmonic measure. The assertion (
15.52
) is now proven.

We conclude as follows. Because of
(
15.51
) and (
15.52
),

(15.60)

\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\frac{\omega^{X}(\Gamma_{2})}{\omega^{X}(\Gamma_{1})}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus 2B.

The bound (
15.50
) - and thus the lemma - follows then from the fact that
\omega^{X}(\Gamma_{2})\lesssim\omega^{X}(\Gamma_{1})
, which is given by Lemma
15.43
.

\Box

Lemma 15.61

(Comparison principle for harmonic measures / Change of poles)
.

Let
B:=B(x_{0},r)
be a ball centered on
\Gamma
and
let
X_{0}
be
a corkscrew point associated to
(x_{0},r)
. Let
E,F\subset\Gamma\cap B
be two Borel subsets of
\Gamma
such that
\omega^{X_{0}}(E)
and
\omega^{X_{0}}(F)
are positive. Then

(15.62)

C^{-1}\frac{\omega^{X_{0}}(E)}{\omega^{X_{0}}(F)}\leq\frac{\omega^{X}(E)}{\omega^{X}(F)}\leq C\frac{\omega^{X_{0}}(E)}{\omega^{X_{0}}(F)}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus 2B,

where
C>0
depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
.
In particular, with the choice
F=B\cap\Gamma
,

(15.63)

C^{-1}\omega^{X_{0}}(E)\leq\frac{\omega^{X}(E)}{\omega^{X}(B\cap\Gamma)}\leq C\omega^{X_{0}}(E)\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus 2B,

where
C>0
depends on the same quantity as for (
15.62
).

Proof
.  This result can be deduced from Lemma
15.47
with the same proof we obtained
[
DFM2
, Lemma 11.135]
from
[
DFM2
, Lemma 11.117]
. It relies on approximations of

X\mapsto\omega^{X}(E)
and
X\mapsto\omega^{X}(F)

by solutions
in
W
to
Lu=0
in
\Omega
.

\Box

Theorem 15.64

(Comparison principle for locally defined functions)
.

There exists
K\geq 2
depending only on
n
,
C_{1}
, and
C_{2}
such that the following holds.

Let
B:=B(x_{0},r)
be a ball centered on
\Gamma
, and let
X_{0}\in\Omega
be a corkscrew point associated to
(x_{0},r)
.
Take
u,v\in W_{r}(KB\cap\overline{\Omega})

to be two non-negative,
not identically zero, solutions to
Lu=Lv=0
in
KB\cap\Omega
,
such that
\Tr u=\Tr v=0
on
KB\cap\Gamma
. Then

(15.65)

C^{-1}\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\leq\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B,

where
C>0
depends only on depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
.

Proof
.  Two strategies can be used to prove this theorem:

(i)

If we mimic the classical proof from the codimension 1 case, we
need to find a (good enough) domain
D
such that
B\cap\Omega\subset D\subset KB\cap\Omega
, and we work with the harmonic measure on
\partial D
of the operator
L
restricted to
D
. One might think that for instance
D=2B\cap\Omega
will work out, but this choice of
D
will not be “good enough” if it is not connected (and it can easily happen).

The difficulty is first to construct such a
D
that satisfies the corkscrew point condition and the Harnack chain condition, but this part could be possibly done by considering the tents sets constructed in Section
5
. Yet, even with such good
D
, we still need to build a measure
\mu_{D}
on
\partial D
which is suitable, in particular satisfies (
H5
) for this particular domain. Well, at the present moment, we don’t even know if building such
\mu_{D}
is possible with our assumptions.

(ii)

The second strategy, that we shall apply, is to follow the ideas used in
[
DFM2
, Theorem 11.146]
. In this strategy, we are not allowed to consider a harmonic measure different from the one we defined on
\Gamma
. The main pitfall in the present theory which did not exist in
[
DFM2
]
is the fact that
\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B)
can be
null because
\Gamma\setminus B
is empty,
and so we do not necessary have the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure, and we shall use the estimate (
15.4
) involving the Green function instead.

We may have chosen to restrict our attention to the balls
B
that do not cover entirely
\Gamma
.
Here we decided to allow more balls, but then when we take
r
large in our theorem - when
\Omega
is unbounded and
\Gamma
is bounded - we need to impose stronger conditions
(and we get a stronger conclusion).

Step 1:
Construction of a
function
f_{y_{0},s}
.

Let
y_{0}\in\Gamma
and
s>0
. We write
Y_{0}
for a corkscrew point associated to
y_{0}
and
s
. Roughly speaking, we would like
to use the function
f_{y_{0},s}(X)
defined by

(15.66)

f_{y_{0},s}(X):=\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})-K_{2}\left[\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},K_{1}s))+\frac{m(B(y_{0},K_{1}s)\cap\Omega)}{(K_{1}s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{K_{1}})\right]

where
Y_{K_{1}}
is a Corkscrew point associated to
y_{0}
and
K_{1}s
,
and where
K_{1},K_{2}>0
are some large constants
that depend only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
. We could show that with large enough choices of
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
,

f_{y_{0},s}
is positive in
B(y_{0},s)
and negative outside of a big ball
B(y_{0},2K_{1}s)
.
However, we want to use the maximum principle to extend such inequalities to larger regions,
and with this definition involving the harmonic measure, our
f_{y_{0},s}
is not smooth enough
to be used in Lemma
14.33
. So we shall first replace

\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},K_{1}s))
in (
15.66
)
by some solution of
Lu=0
with smooth Dirichlet condition.

Let
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be such that
0\leq h\leq 1
,
h\equiv 0
on
B(0,1/2)
and

h\equiv 1
on the complement of
B(0,1)
.
For
\beta>1

(which will be chosen large), we define
h_{\beta}
by
h_{\beta}(x)=h(\frac{x-y_{0}}{\beta s})
.
Let
u_{\beta}
be the solution, given by Lemma
12.2
,
of
Lu_{\beta}=0
with the Dirichlet condition
\Tr u_{\beta}=\Tr h_{\beta}
.
Notice that
u_{\beta}\in W
because
1-u_{\beta}
is the solution of
L
with the smooth and compactly supported trace of

1-h_{\beta}
.
By the positivity of the harmonic measure,
it holds that
for any
X\in\Omega
and
\gamma>0
,

(15.67)

\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},\beta s))\leq u_{\beta}(X)\leq\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},\beta s/2)),

and we can see here that
u_{\beta}
will be used as a smooth substitute of the harmonic measure.

Similarly to (
15.32
), using the Green function upper bounds and (
H4
), we have

(15.68)

\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\leq C\qquad\ \text{ for }X\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4).

Then by
Lemma
14.33
with
E=\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)
and
F=\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\overline{B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)}
,

(15.69)

\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\leq C\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4).

From this and
the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure (Lemma
15.1
), we deduce
that for
\beta\in(1,\infty)
and
X\in\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},2\beta s)

(15.70)

\begin{split}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})&\leq K_{2}\left[\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus B(y_{0},\beta s))+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta}))\right]\\
&\leq K_{2}\left[u_{\beta}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta}))\right],\end{split}

where
Y_{\beta}
is a corkscrew point associated to
(y_{0},\beta s)
, and where the constant
K_{2}>0
depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
; in particular,
K_{2}
does not depend on
\beta
.

Our aim now is to find
K_{1}\geq 20C_{1}
such that, for

X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)]
,

(15.71)

K_{2}\left[u_{K_{1}}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},K_{1}s)\cap\Omega)}{(K_{1}s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{K_{1}})\right]\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0}).

According to the Hölder continuity at the boundary (Lemma
11.32
), we have

(15.72)

\sup_{B(y_{0},10s)}u_{\beta}\leq C\beta^{-\alpha}.

Moreover, Lemma
14.83

applied to
g(.,Y_{\beta})
implies, that

(15.73)

\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta})\leq C\beta^{-\alpha}\qquad\text{ for }X\in B(y_{0},10s).

If both cases, we need
\beta
to be big enough, for instance
\beta\geq 20C_{1}
, and the constants
C
and
\alpha>0
depend only
on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
.
Due to the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure given by (
15.4
) - and (
H4
) - we have

u_{4}+\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\geq C^{-1}\quad\text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},5s).

By
the last estimate in (
15.72
)–(
15.73
),
there exists

K_{3}>0
such that

(15.74)

u_{\beta}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta})\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\left[u_{4}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\right]

for

X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},10s)\setminus B(y_{0},5s)]
. In addition, by increasing
K_{3}
if needed,
the estimates (
15.72
)–(
15.73
) and the
lower bound in (
14.65
) for the Green function imply that

(15.75)

u_{\beta}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta})\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})

when
X\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)
. We invoke then Lemma
14.33
, used on the function

X\to K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\left[u_{4}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\right]-\left[u_{\beta}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta})\right]

and the sets
E=B(y_{0},10s)\setminus\overline{B}(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)
and

F=\overline{B(y_{0},5s)}\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)
, to deduce that
for
X\in\Omega\cap B(y_{0},10s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)
,

\begin{split}u_{\beta}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},\beta s)\cap\Omega)}{(\beta s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{\beta})&\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\left[u_{4}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\right]\\
&\leq K_{3}\beta^{-\alpha}\left[\omega^{X}(B(y_{0},4s))+\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\right]\\
&\leq C\beta^{-\alpha}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})\end{split}

by Lemma
15.28
. Therefore, (
15.71
) can be indeed achieved for some large
K_{1}
, that depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
(recall that
K_{2}
is already fixed, and depends on the same parameters).

Define the function
f_{y_{0},s}
on
\Omega\setminus\{Y_{0}\}
by

(15.76)

f(X):=\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})-K_{2}\left[u_{K_{1}}(X)+\frac{m(B(y_{0},K_{1}s)\cap\Omega)}{(K_{1}s)^{2}}g(X,Y_{K_{1}}))\right].

The inequality (
15.70
) gives

(15.77)

f_{y_{0},s}(X)\leq 0\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{1}s),

and the estimate (
15.71
) proves
that

(15.78)

f_{y_{0},s}(X)\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(X,Y_{0})

for
X\in\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)]
.

Step 2:
End of the proof

Let us turn to the proof of the comparison principle.
By symmetry and as
in Lemma
15.47
, it suffices to prove the upper bound in (
15.65
), that is

(15.79)

\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap B,\text{ where }B=B(x_{0},r).

We claim that

(15.80)

v(X)\geq C^{-1}\frac{m(B)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(X,X_{0})\qquad\text{ for }X\in[\Omega\cap B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4),

where
C>0
depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
.
So let
X\in\Omega\cap B
be given. Two cases may happen.
If
\delta(X)\geq\frac{r}{8K_{1}}
, where
K_{1}
comes from (
15.71
)
and is the same as in the definition of
f_{y_{0},s}
, the
existence of Harnack chains (Proposition
2.18
) and the Harnack inequality (Lemma
11.35
) give that

v(X)\approx v(X_{0})

For the above inequality to hold, we need the Harnack chains to stay in the area where
v
is a solution; we take
K
big enough to make sure that it happens, and by Proposition
2.18
,
K
need to depend only on
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.
Similarly, Proposition
2.18
and Lemma
11.35
,
together with the bound (
14.65
) on the Green function,
give that

\frac{m(B)}{r^{2}}g(X,X_{0})\approx 1\qquad\text{ on }[\Omega\cap B(x_{0},r)]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4).

We conclude that for all
X\in[\Omega\cap B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
satisfying
\delta(X)\geq\frac{r}{8K_{1}}
.

(15.81)

v(X)\approx v(X_{0})\frac{m(B)}{r^{2}}g(X,X_{0}).

The more interesting remaining case is when

\delta(X)<\frac{r}{8K_{1}}
.
Take
y_{0}\in\Gamma
such that
|X-y_{0}|=\delta(X)
. Set

s:=\frac{r}{8K_{1}}
and
Y_{0}
a corkscrew point associated to
(y_{0},s)
.
The ball
B(y_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)=B(y_{0},8K_{1}s)
is contained
in
B(x_{0},\frac{7}{4}r)
. The following points hold :

•

The quantity
\int_{B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}|\nabla v|^{2}dm
is finite
because
v\in W_{r}(B(x_{0},2r))
. The fact that

\int_{B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}|\nabla f_{y_{0},s}|^{2}dm
is finite as well
follows from the property (
14.61
) of the Green function.

•

There exists
K_{4}>0
such that

(15.82)

K_{4}v(Y)-v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq 0\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4).

This latter inequality is due to the following two bounds: the fact that

(15.83)

f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\leq\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}g(Y,Y_{0})\leq C\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4),

which is a consequence of
the definition (
15.76
) and
(
14.64
), and the bound

(15.84)

v(Y)\geq C^{-1}v(Y_{0})\ \ \ \text{ for }Y\in B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2),

which comes from the Harnack inequality (Lemma
11.35
).

•

The function
K_{4}v-v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}

is nonnegative on

\Omega\cap[B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{1}s)]
. Indeed,
v\geq 0
on
\Omega\cap B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)
and, thanks to (
15.77
),
f_{y_{0},s}\leq 0
on
\Omega\setminus B(y_{0},2K_{1}s)
.

•

The trace of
K_{4}v-v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}
is non-negative on

B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\cap\Gamma

again because
\Tr v=0
on

B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\cap\Gamma

and
\Tr[f_{y_{0},s}]\leq 0
on

B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\cap\Gamma

by construction.

The previous points prove that
K_{4}v-v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}
satisfies the assumptions of
Lemma
14.33
with

E=B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\setminus\overline{B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)}
and
F=\overline{B(y_{0},2K_{1}s)}\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/2)
. As a consequence, for any
Y\in B(y_{0},4K_{1}s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)

(15.85)

K_{4}v(Y)-v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq 0,

and hence,
for any
Y\in B(y_{0},s)\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4)

(15.86)

v(Y)\geq(K_{4})^{-1}v(Y_{0})f_{y_{0},s}(Y)\geq C^{-1}\frac{m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)}{s^{2}}v(Y_{0})g(Y,Y_{0})

by
(
15.78
). The points
X_{0}
and
Y_{0}
are both corkscrew points, and they can be linked by a Harnack chain of balls whose length depends only on
r/s=8K_{1}
, that is with uniformly bounded length. So using the
Harnack inequality on every ball of the chain,
we deduce that
v(Y_{0})\approx v(X_{0})
and
g(Y,Y_{0})\approx g(Y,X_{0})
whenever
Y\in B(y_{0},s)
is far from
Y_{0}
and
X_{0}
(but it cannot be close to
X_{0}
since
K_{1}\geq 20C_{1}
). Moreover,
s^{-2}m(B(y_{0},s)\cap\Omega)\approx r^{-2}m(B\cap\Omega)
by (
H4
). Therefore (
15.85
) becomes

v(Y)\geq C^{-1}\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(Y,X_{0})\qquad\text{ for }Y\in[\Omega\cap B(y_{0},s)]\setminus B(Y_{0},\delta(Y_{0})/4).

Since the two functions of
Y
in the inequality above are solutions in
\Omega\cap B(y_{0},2s)
, the Harnack inequality yields the following improvement:

v(Y)\geq C^{-1}\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(Y,X_{0})\qquad\text{ for }Y\in\Omega\cap B(y_{0},s).

Recall that
X\in B(y_{0},s)
by construction of
y_{0}
and
s
. We conclude, at last, that even when
X\in\Omega\cap B
is such that
\delta(X)<\frac{r}{8K_{1}}
, we still have

v(X)\geq C^{-1}\frac{m(B)}{r^{2}}v(X_{0})g(X,X_{0}).

The claim (
15.80
) follows.

Now we want to prove that, for all
X\in[\Omega\cap B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
,

(15.87)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\left[\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus\frac{5}{4}B)+\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}g(.,X_{0})\right],

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
n
,
C_{1}
to
C_{6}
, and
C_{A}
.
By Lemma
15.14
,

(15.88)

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\cap\frac{7}{4}B,

as long as
K
is large enough so that Lemma
15.14
can be applied.
But again,
K
does not need to depend on anything else than

n
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.
Pick
h^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that
0\leq h^{\prime}\leq 1
,
h^{\prime}\equiv 1
outside of
\frac{3}{2}B
,
and
h^{\prime}\equiv 0
on
\frac{5}{4}B
.
Let
u_{h^{\prime}}=U(h^{\prime})
be the solution of
Lu_{h^{\prime}}=0
with
the data
\Tr u_{h^{\prime}}=\Tr h^{\prime}

(given by Lemma
12.2
).
As before,
u_{h^{\prime}}\in W
because
1-u_{h^{\prime}}=U(1-h)
and
1-h
is a test function.
Also,
u_{h^{\prime}}(X)\geq\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus\frac{3}{2}B)
by monotonicity.
So (
15.4
), which
states the non-degeneracy of the harmonic measure, gives

(15.89)

u_{h^{\prime}}(X)+\frac{m(\frac{3}{2}B\cap\Omega)}{(\frac{3}{2}r)^{2}}g(X,X_{3/2})\geq C^{-1}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus\frac{13}{8}B,

where
X_{3/2}
is a corkscrew point associated to
(x_{0},\frac{3}{2}r)
. The
doubling property (
H4
) and Harnack inequality for the function
g(X,.)=g_{T}(.,X)
entail now that

(15.90)

u_{h^{\prime}}(X)+\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}g(X,X_{0})\geq C^{-1}\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in\Omega\setminus\frac{13}{8}B.

The combination of (
15.88
) and (
15.90
) yields the existence of
K_{5}>0
such that

\tilde{u}:=K_{5}u(X_{0})\left[u_{h^{\prime}}+\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}g(.,X_{0})\right]-u\geq 0\quad\text{ on }\Omega\cap[\frac{7}{4}B\setminus\frac{13}{8}B].

Moreover, using the Harnack inequality and the Green function lower bounds, by increasing slightly
K_{5}
if needed, we also have
\tilde{u}\geq 0
in
B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/2)\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
. Now, it is not very hard to see that
\tilde{u}
satisfies all the assumptions of Lemma
14.33
, with the sets
E=\frac{7}{4}B\setminus\overline{B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)}
and
F=\overline{\frac{13}{8}B}\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/2)
.
Observe in particular that
u_{h^{\prime}}\in W
,
Tu_{h^{\prime}}\geq 0
, and as long as we choose
K\geq 2
,
u\in W_{r}(2B)
and
Tu=0
on
\Gamma\cap 2B
.
Then by Lemma
14.33
,

(15.91)

u(X)\leq K_{5}u(X_{0})\left[u_{h^{\prime}}(X)+\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}g(X,X_{0})\right]\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in[\Omega\cap\frac{7}{4}B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4),

and since
u_{h^{\prime}}(X)\leq\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus\frac{5}{4}B)

for all
X\in\Omega
,

u(X)\leq Cu(X_{0})\left[\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus\frac{5}{4}B)+\frac{m(B\cap\Omega)}{r^{2}}g(X,X_{0})\right]\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in[\Omega\cap\frac{7}{4}B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4).

The claim (
15.87
) follows.

The bounds (
15.80
) and (
15.87
) imply that

(15.92)

\frac{u(X)}{v(X)}\leq C\frac{u(X_{0})}{v(X_{0})}\left[\frac{r^{2}\omega^{X}(\Gamma\setminus\frac{5}{4}B)}{m(B\cap\Omega)g(X,X_{0})}+1\right]\ \ \ \text{ for }X\in[\Omega\cap B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4).

The bound (
15.79
) in the set
[\Omega\cap B]\setminus B(X_{0},\delta(X_{0})/4)
is now a consequence of the above inequality and (
15.30
). The bound (
15.79
) in the full domain
\Omega\cap B
is then an easy consequence of the Harnack inequality (Lemma
11.35
).

\Box
